## Reviewer's notes

- **What this row is.** The first row of the model matrix (`docs/dev/ai_analyst_shell.md`,
  3.4): the open-weight finalist of the owner's October to November 2026 evaluation, through
  Goose with the toolkit's MCP server alone. The toolkit was develop at the merge of PR #2957
  plus PR #2961 and the driver branch, merged locally on the host; the toolkit commits the
  header names are those local merges, not commits on GitHub. Its 16 tools are the surface
  before `reduce` (PR #2983).
- **Three runs, one row.** The first run (started 19:04 UTC) ran all three repetitions in 85
  minutes until the provider began answering 529 "System is overloaded" at about 20:15 UTC,
  which took most of the third repetition. The second run (20:56 UTC) tried the fourteen
  episodes again and got two through; the third (the next day, 12:05 UTC) ran the twelve
  left. The earlier attempts of an episode are infra and superseded by the later run's record.
- **A caution on the analysis class.** The recipe of this row told the model to call `reduce`,
  which this toolkit did not have (the driver now gives that hint only when the server lists
  the tool). The metadata, interpretation and decline classes do not depend on it; the
  analysis class was told of a tool it could not call, on top of having no computation path
  through MCP.
- **An earlier attempt of this row (15:06 UTC the same day) is not here.** Its token was
  revoked at 16:29 UTC when a side arm minted the demo account's ai-agent token; everything it
  recorded after that point was a 401. The driver now holds a lock so one run at a time holds
  the stack, and reads a refused token as infra.
- **Side arms**, same model, stack and questions: `2026-11-05-nemotron-3.5-super-vl-goose-reduce-arm.md` (MCP with the
  `reduce` tool, A1, A4, A6, two repetitions) and `2026-11-05-nemotron-3.5-super-vl-goose-mcp-python-arm.md` (MCP plus Goose's
  developer extension, a shell, A1 to A6, two repetitions).
- **Hand verdicts.** None changed: the checker's readings (a number written as text, a null
  beside the asked fields, nulls where values were asked read as an abstention, a reordered
  list, an m/z rounded within 1 ppm, the solver's own suggestions) are named in the Checker
  column, and the reviewer agrees with each. The two wrong analysis answers stand: A1 rep 2
  names a background sample as the brightest (a false value), and A6 rep 0 declined inside a
  JSON string in the answer field, the model's own JSON error, which is not an abstention.
